# Financial Management: question papers in full

102 unique papers · 2008-09 to 2025-26 · Common (all branches), Information Technology, Chemical, Computer Science and Business Systems, Computer Engineering, Artificial Intelligence, Data Science, Electronics and Telecommunication, Mechanical, Manufacturing, Real Estate and Construction Management

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

Q.2   Solitaire Cart Ltd produces eco-friendly disposable packaging   [20]
CO -3   solutions for manufacturers and wants to install a Machine for
BL   designing modern range of products, for this purpose it is
Analyze   considering two alternative models R and S.
Machine R is a semi-automatic machine and is available at a cost
of Rs. 4,00,000. The machine has a 5-year life with 10% Residual
Value. The annual volume of production is estimated at 1,50,000
units, which can be sold at 6 per unit. Annual variable costs are
estimated at Rs. 2 per unit (excluding depreciation) and fixed cost
is estimated to be Rs. 3,00,000.
Another model is fully automatic Machine S, capable of giving an
output of 1,50,000 units and will sell at 6 per unit, however annual
variable cost for machine S is Rs. 1.8 per unit (excluding
depreciation). There will be no change in Fixed Cost. Machine S
costs Rs. 4,50,000, its Residual Value will be 10% after a useful
life of 5 years.
Working Capital of Rs. 50,000 would be required. Company
follows straight line method of depreciation, and it will be in the
tax bracket of 25%. Cost of Capital of the company is 15%.
Help the management of Solitaire Cart Ltd in selecting one of the
two Machines?
Support your view with necessary workings.

Q2. The United Petroleum Ltd (UPL) has a retail outlet of petrol, diesel and petroleum products. Presently,
it has two pumps exclusively for petrol, one for non-lead petrol and one for diesel. Free air filling is carried
out for vehicles buying fuel from the outlet. The pumps have a useful life of 10 years with no salvage value
as the underground tank will be completely corroded and unfit for reuse.

The UPL sells petrol and diesel @ Rs 23 and Rs 10 per liter respectively. The existing annual sale is petrol,
5 lakh liters, and diesel, 2 lakh liters. Its earnings are 4 per cent as commission on sales. Due to a manifold
increase in traffic, the existing pumps are not able to meet the demand during peak hours. The UPL is
contemplating installation of additional pumps for diesel and petrol at a cost of Rs 10,00,000 together with
additional working capital of Rs 5,00,000. The additional sales of petrol and diesel are expectethto be 2 lakh
liters and 1 lakh liters per annum respectively. As a result of the installation of the new pump, the operating
cost would increase by Rs 24,000 annually by way of salary of the pump operator. Other yearly associated
additional costs are estimated to be: insurance @ | per cent of the cost of the pump, maintenance cost, Rs
12,000 and power costs, Rs 13,000. United Petroleum Ltd pays 35 per cent on tax on its income.
Depreciation will be on straight line basis and the same is allowed for tax purposes.

The management of UPL seeks your advice on the financial viability of the expansion proposal. Prepare a
report for its consideration, assuming 12 per cent required rate of return. (14 Marks)

structure has a debt of Rs 25 crores with cost of 10%. The cost of equity is reckoned at 15%. It has earnings
level of Rs 5 crores and does not pay any taxes being tax-exempt unit. The Managing Director of the firm
believes that the level of debt is too high and would like to repay the debt by issuing additional shares to the
extent of Rs 10 crore. According to him reduced level of leverage would reduce the cost of capital because
of two reasons - cost of debt falling from existing 10% to 9%, and cost of equity falling from 15% to 14%.

the firm in both the situations. (14 Marks)

Q. 2 A company is considering the replacement of its existing machine which is obsolete and
unable to meet the rapidly rising demand for its product. The company is faced with two
alternatives: (12 Marks)
(i) To buy Machine A which is similar to the existing machine or

(ii) To go in for Machine B which is more expensive and has much greater capacity.

The cash flows at the present level of operations under the two alternatives are as follows:
Year 0 1 2 3 4 5

Machine A -25 0 5 20 14 14

Machine B -40 10 14 16 17 15

The company’s cost of capital is 10%. The finance manager tries to evaluate the machines by
calculating the following:

i) Net Present Value

ii) Profitability Index’

iii) Payback period

iv) Discounted Payback period

At the end of his calculations, however, the finance manager is unable to make up his mind as to
which machine to recommend, You are required to make these calculation and in the light thereof

c. Rs 25,000 p.a. in perpetuity

d. Rs 10,000 per month for a year and Rs 1,00,000 at the end of the year

e. Rs 25,000 per year for the next 10 years.

2. A project is costing Rs 120 crore. The life of the project is 8 years. The annual cost including depreciation is Rs 50 crore. If
the required rate of return is 15% find out what minimum revenue the project must generate to make it viable. Assume
depreciation at SLM and tax rate of 40%. (12)

3. (12)
MangalDass Enterprises is in the business of manufacture and export of garments. Currently in its capital structure it has a
debt of Rs 15 crores with cost of 10%. The cost of equity is reckoned at 15%. It has earnings level of Rs 3.75 crores and does
not pay any taxes being tax-exempt unit. The Managing Director of the firm believes that the level of debt is too high and
would like to repay the debt by issuing additional shares to the extent of Rs 5 crore. According to him reduced level of
leverage would reduce the cost of capital because of two reasons - cost of debt falling from existing 10% to 9%, and cost of
equity falling from 15% to 14%. Evaluate the option of the Managing Director to replace debt with equity. Also find the
WACC and value of the firm in both the situations.

4. a) ABC Ltd. has expected earnings at Rs 30 per share of which it expects to distribute Rs 15 as dividend. Dividend has been
growing at the rate of 15% previously. The market price of its share is Rs 300.

Find the following:

i) Current cost of equity (3)
ii) Cost of new equity if the firm issues fresh shares at current market price but with floatation cost of 5% (3)
b) What do you understand by business risk? How can this be measures? (6)

Y2
--- page 2 ---
5 Ke = \>"I

MultiactivityLtd is has series of projects that can generate returns of 10%, 12% and 14%. Currently it has earnings of Rs 12
per share of which 25% is the minimum dividend payout has to be maintained due to investors preference for current income.
The managing director of the firm is considering three alternative dividend policies of 25%, 50% and 75% payout.

i) What is the likely impact on the value of the share of dividend policy under different rein’
ii) What conclusions can you draw?

(12)

vestment rate under Walter Model?

6. What are the major forms of business? Describe in brief. (12)

7. Discuss the types of Financial Markets and their relationship. (12)

4. Marito Private Limited has 2,00,000 outstanding shares selling at INR 50 each. The firm has
net profits of INR 20,00,000 and intends to make new investments of INR 40,00,000 during the current
financial year. The cost of capital of the firm is 10%. (4*3=12 Marks)

Determine the price of the equity at the end of the current under the following conditions:

a. Dividend is declared and the dividend payable per share is INR 15.

b. Dividend is not declared.

a. Mr Raj is about to retire at the age of 60. His employer has offered him two post retirement

which is a better option? (3)

2 Rs 2,50,000 | Rs 2,50,000 6 1

c. Star Company earns Rs 8 per share, is capitalised at a rate of 12% and has rate of return on
investment 20%. According to Walter’s Model what should be the price per share at 25%
dividend pay-out ratio: Is this the Optimum Pay-out ratio as per Walter’s Model? (3)

d. Determine the cost of Equity of a company if the risk free rate of return is 8 %, required rate of
return on market portfolio is 13% and the firm’s beta is 1.6. (3)

Planet Ltd had 50,000 equity shares of Rs 10 each outstanding on January 1. The shares are currently
being quoted at par in the market. The company now intends to pay a dividend of Rs 2 per share for
the current calendar year. It belongs to a risk class whose appropriate capitalization rate is 15 %.

Using the Modigliani- Miller model and assuming no taxes, ascertain the price of the company’s share
as it is likely to prevail at the end of the year (a) when the dividend is declared and (b) when no dividend
is declared (c) Also find out the number of new equity shares that the company must issue to meet its
investment needs of Rs 2 Lakh, assuming a net income of Rs 1.1 lakh and also assuming that the
dividend is paid. f

Q2. B. Explain the MM hypothesis of dividend Policy (06)

A hospital is considering to purchase a diagnostic machine costing Rs 80,000. The projected life of the
machine is 8 years and has an expected salvage value of Rs 6,000 at the end of 8 years. The annual
operating cost of the machine is Rs 7,500. It is expected to generate revenues of Rs 40,000 per year
for 8 years. Presently the hospital is outsourcing the diagnostic work and is earning a commission
income of Rs 12,000 p.a net of taxes.

Would it profitable for the hospital to purchase the machine? Company is in 30% tax bracket. Give
your recommendations under NPV method and Profitability Index assuming 10% discounting rate.

The next expected Dividend on Equity shares per share is Rs 3.60 per share; the dividend per share is
expected to grow at the rate of 7%. The market price is Rs 40.

Preference stock, redeemable after 10 years is currently selling at Rs 75 per share.

Debenture, redeemable after 6 years are selling at Rs 80 per debenture.

The income tax rate for the company is.40 %.

Calculate WACC using Market Value Weights.

- Departments: Common (all branches)
- Programmes: MBA TECH
- Source: `02_Question_Papers/Legacy/MBA TECH/4TH YEAR/COMMON FOR ALL BRANCH VIII/SEM VIII/FINANCIAL MANAGEMENT/Financial Management Batch (2013-14) Re Exam.pdf`

- Text: ocr (2 pages), mean OCR confidence 93.2

````text
SVKM’s NMIMS
MUKESH PATEL SCHOOL OF TECHNOLOGY MANAGEMENT & ENGINEERING

Programme: MBA Tech (All Streams) Year: IV Semester: VIII

Academic Year: 2014-15
Batch: 2013-14

Subject: Financial Management

Marks: 50 i es
Date: 08/07/2015 Time: 10.08 %p = 12.00 noot Jj
Durations: Roc) 7

* aye — eee

Re-Examination

Instruction: Candidates should read carefully the instructions printed on the question paper
and on the cover of the Answer Book, which is provided for their use.

NB:

1) Question No. Q1 is compulsory.

2) Out of remaining questions, attempt any 4 questions.
3) In all Q5 hi to be attempted.

4) All questions,10 equal marks.

5) Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.

Que.1 A company invests Rs. 13,00,000 in a project which has a life of five years. The
inflows are Rs. 3,00,000, Rs. 4,00,000, Rs. 3,50,000, Rs. 5,00,000 and Rs. 2,50,000 for years
1,2,3,4 and 5 respectively. Find the payback period, NPV and IRR of this project, assuming
cost of capital of 10%. Marks 10

Que. 2 Two firms A and B are identical in all respects except the degree of leverage. Firm A
has 6% debt of Rs. 3,00,000, while firm B has no debt. Both the firms earning an EBT of
Rs.1,20,000 each. The equity capitalisation rate is 10% and tlhe corporate tax rate is 60%.
Compute the market value of the two firms. Marks 10

Que.3 Following are the details regarding three companies A Itd, B ltd and C Itd:

A ltd B ltd C ltd Marks 10
r=15% r=5% r= 10%

Ke= 10% K.= 10% Ke= 10%

E=Rs. 12 E=Rs. 12 E=Rs. 12

Calculate the value of an equity share of each of these companies applying Walter’s formula
when dividend payment ratio (D/P ratio) is: a) 50% b) 75% c) 25%

Que. 4 “It is the capital expenditure decision that spells the difference between the business
success and business failure”? Do you agree with the statement? Substantiate your views with
reasons. Marks 10

4/2
--- page 2 ---
Que. 5 A new project under construction, your company requires a capital investment of Rs.
150 lakhs. Interest on term loan is 12% and tax rate is 50%. If the debt equity ratio insisted by
the financing agencies is 2:1, calculate the point of indifference for the project. | Marks 10

Que. 6 “An optimal combination of the decisions relating to investment, financing and
dividends will maximise the value of the firm to its shareholders.” Examine. Marks 10

Que.7. Amol wishes to retire 20 years from now and expects to live for 15 years thereafter.
He wishes to have a yearly pension of Rs. 2,00,000 for 15 years he will live after retirement.
He wants the pension to start one year after retirement. If the interest rate is 8% and expected
to remain same, what lump sum amount should Amol set aside today to facilitate this?

Marks 10
````

Instruction: Candidates should read carefully the instructions printed on the question paper and

NB:

1) Question No. _1___ is compulsory.

2) Out of remaining questions, attempt any _ 4 questions.
3) Inall_5____ questions to be attempted.

4) All questions carry equal marks.

5) Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
6) Figures in brackets.on the right hand side indicate full marks.
7) Assume Suitable data if necessary

QI. a) Identify the source for identifying a promising investment opportunity and the nature of investment

decision by citing a corporate example. (3)
b) What is an optimum capital structure? What do the capital structure theories refer to? (3)
c) “Money has time value” Explain with Example. (3)
d) Calculate the required rate of return on equity from the given information. (3)
Dividend paid for the last 4 years are as follows: :
Year 1 2 [3 4 “|
Dividend 1.25 1.35 | 4.00 4.10

Current Market Price is Rs 25

Q2).S. Ltd manufactures shoes. Mr. D, president is considering the company’s ordering, inventory and
billing procedures. He estimates that the annual savings from computerization include a reduction
of 10 clerical staff with annual salaries of Rs 15000/- each, Rs 8000/- from reduced production
delays caused by inventory problems, Rs 12000/- from lost sales due to inventory stock outs and
Rs 3000/-associated with timely billing procedures. The purchase price of the system is Rs
200,000/- and installation costs are Rs 50000/-. These outlays will be capitalized (depreciated) on
a straight line basis to zero book salvage value which is also its market value at the end of the 5
years. Operation of the system-requires 2 computer specialists with annual salaries of Rs 40,000/-
per person. Also annual maintenance and operating (cash) expenses of Rs 12000/- are estimated to
be required. The company’s tax rate is 40% and cost of capital for this project is 12%.

You are required to- (12)
i) " . Find the project’s initial net cash outlay:

ii) Find the project’s operating and terminal value cash flows over its 5 year life.

iii) Evaluate the project using NPV method.

iv) Calculate the project’s payback period.
--- page 2 ---
Q3) The following information has been extracted from the Balance sheet of F Ltd as on 31.12.2014:

Capital Rs in lacs
Equity Share Capital 400
12% Debentures 400
18% Term loan 1200
2000

a) Determine the Weighted Average Cost of Capital of the company. It has been paying dividends at a

consistent rate of 20% per annum.
b) What difference will it make ifthe current price of the Rs 100 share is Rs 160/-
c) Determine the effect of Income tax on the cost of capital under both premises (Tax rate 40%) (12)

Q 4.What is lease? How it is beneficial to the firm? Discuss various types of lease. (12)

Q5.a) Comment on the emerging role of the finance manager in India. (10)

b) Mr. X. deposits Rs 2000 at the end of every year for 5 years in his saving account paying 5%
interest compounded annually. He wants to determine how much sum of money he will have at the

end of the 5 th year. (2)

Q6) A textile company belongs to a risk class for which the appropriate P/E ratio is 10. It currently has
50000 outstanding shares selling at Rs 100 each. The company is contemplating the declaration of
Rs 8 dividend at the end of the current fiscal year which has just started. Given the assumption of

MM, answer the following questions:-

i) What will the price of the share be at the end of the year: a) if dividend is not declared and b) if
itis declared? (4)

ii)Assuming that the firm pays the dividend, has a net income (y) of Rs 5,00,000 and makes a new
investment of Rs. 10,00,000 during the period, how many new share must be issued? (4)

iii) What will the value of the firm be: a) if dividend is declared b) if dividend is not declared? (4)

Q7) Company X & Y are in the same risk class, and are identical in every respect except that Company
X uses debt, while company Y does not. The levered firm has Rs 9,00,000 debentures carrying
10% rate of interest. Both the companies earn 20% operating profit on their total assets of Rs. 15
lakhs. Assume perfect markets, rational investors etc., and a tax rate of 35% and capitalisation rate

of 15% for an all equity company. -

a) Compute the value of the companies X & Y using Net Income (NI) Approach. (3)
b) Compute the value of the companies X & Y using Net Operating Income (NOI) Approach. (3)

c) Using the NOI Approach, calculate the overall cost of capital (k,) for both the companies X & Y. (3)

d) Which of these two companies has an optimal capital structure according to NOI approach?
(3)

Instruction: 1) Each question should be started on fresh page
2) Each question carries 10 marks.

PARTA
Q1 is compulsory and attempt any two from remaining three

Q1) Super Ltd. is considering three financing plans:

Financial Plans Equity Debt Preference
A 100% - -
B 50% 50% -
Cc 50% - 50%

- Total funds to be raised — Rs. 200 crore.

- Rate of interest on debt — 12%.

- Corporate tax rate — 35%.

- Dividend on preference shares — 9%.

- Face value of equity shares — Rs. 10 each. These shares will be issued at a premium of Rs. 10
per share.

- Expected EBIT - Rs. 80 crore.

Determine:
(i) EPS and financial break-even point for each plan.
(ii) Indifference points between financial plans A and B; and A and C.

Q2) Bestbuy Auto Ltd. has outstanding 1,20,000 shares selling at Rs. 20 per share. The company

hopes to make a net income of Rs. 350,000 during the year ended 31% March, 2003. The

company is considering to pay a dividend of Rs. 2 per share at the end of current year. The

following information is available

value. It has a remaining life of 5 years after which, on disposal, it
would fetch a value equal to its then book value
b. The new machine costs Rs. 16,00,000. The depreciation rate for this will be
33 & 1/3% After 5 years it is expected to fetch a value equal to its
book value. The replacement of old machine should increase revenues
by Rs. 2,00,000 per year and reduce operating cost (excluding
depreciation) by Rs. 1,50,000 per year.
Compute the incremental post tax cash flows associated with the replacement
proposal assuming a tax rate of 50%
(10Marks)
